==== 1. listener POST /glyph/create reports the Unicode of the new glyph ====
1a  'B'  (standard)                    -> 200 {'created': True, 'unicode': 66, 'unicode_status': 'assigned'}
1b  'eacute' (Adobe Glyph List name)   -> 200 {'created': True, 'unicode': 233, 'unicode_status': 'assigned'}
1c  'zzcontrol', FontLab says 0        -> 200 {'created': True, 'unicode': None, 'unicode_status': 'none'}
1d  'zzcontrol', FontLab says None     -> 200 {'created': True, 'unicode': None, 'unicode_status': 'none'}
1e  'B', reading .unicode raises       -> 200 {'created': True, 'unicode': None, 'unicode_status': 'unreadable', 'unicode_error': 'unicode read failed'}
1f  createGlyph does nothing           -> 200 {'created': False, 'reason': 'createGlyph() did not raise, but the glyph is not in font.glyphs afterward'}
1g  name already exists                -> 200 {'created': False, 'reason': 'a glyph with this name already exists'}
1j  e-acute at the end of a name (stripped)   -> 200 {'created': False, 'created_under_other_names': ['eacut'], 'reason': 'createGlyph() did not raise, but FontLab created a glyph under a different name instead'}
1k  a lone e-acute (name becomes EMPTY)       -> 200 {'created': False, 'created_under_other_names': [''], 'reason': 'createGlyph() did not raise, but FontLab created a glyph under a different name instead'}
1l  ...and an empty-named glyph already there -> 200 {'created': False, 'created_under_other_names': [''], 'reason': 'createGlyph() did not raise, but FontLab created a glyph under a different name instead'}
1m  'a b' (space stripped)                    -> 200 {'created': False, 'created_under_other_names': ['ab'], 'reason': 'createGlyph() did not raise, but FontLab created a glyph under a different name instead'}
1n  B, scan 66, B.alt already holds 66        -> 200 {'created': True, 'unicode': None, 'unicode_status': 'none', 'duplicate_check': {'scanned': 1, 'also_held_by': ['B.alt'], 'unreadable': 0}}
1o  B, scan 66, nobody holds it (assigned)    -> 200 {'created': True, 'unicode': 66, 'unicode_status': 'assigned'}
1p  zzcontrol, scan 66 (none, nobody holds)   -> 200 {'created': True, 'unicode': None, 'unicode_status': 'none', 'duplicate_check': {'scanned': 1, 'also_held_by': [], 'unreadable': 0}}
1q  zzcontrol, scan 65: A is the holder       -> 200 {'created': True, 'unicode': None, 'unicode_status': 'none', 'duplicate_check': {'scanned': 1, 'also_held_by': ['A'], 'unreadable': 0}}
1r  zzcontrol, no scan_unicode field          -> 200 {'created': True, 'unicode': None, 'unicode_status': 'none'}
1s  scan_unicode True (ignored)               -> 200 {'created': True, 'unicode': None, 'unicode_status': 'none'}
1t  scan_unicode 0 (ignored)                  -> 200 {'created': True, 'unicode': None, 'unicode_status': 'none'}
1u  scan_unicode '66' (ignored)               -> 200 {'created': True, 'unicode': None, 'unicode_status': 'none'}
1v  scan_unicode 1114112 (ignored)            -> 200 {'created': True, 'unicode': None, 'unicode_status': 'none'}
1h  no font open                       -> (200, {'open': False})
1i  /version                           -> {'version': '0.200'}

-- a glyph appeared under another name (the server's message)
2ze  ['eacut'] -> Glyph 'a-b' was NOT created, but FontLab DID create a glyph under a different name instead: 'eacut'. It strips characters it does not accept from names. That glyph is now in the font: tell the person; delete_glyph can remove it. The font HAS been modified by this, so it counts as a change: when your task package is finished, call finish_changes once so the person is reminded to save.
2ze  [''] -> Glyph 'a-b' was NOT created, but FontLab DID create a glyph under a different name instead: an EMPTY name. It strips characters it does not accept from names. That glyph is now in the font: tell the person; a glyph with an empty name cannot be deleted with delete_glyph, which needs a name. The font HAS been modified by this, so it counts as a change: when your task package is finished, call finish_changes once so the person is reminded to save.
2ze  ['', 'x'] -> Glyph 'a-b' was NOT created, but FontLab DID create a glyph under a different name instead: an EMPTY name, 'x'. It strips characters it does not accept from names. That glyph is now in the font: tell the person; a glyph with an empty name cannot be deleted with delete_glyph, which needs a name. The font HAS been modified by this, so it counts as a change: when your task package is finished, call finish_changes once so the person is reminded to save.

19c instruction                  : Values are in font units (units_per_em says how many per em); a NEGATIVE value moves the pair closer together, a positive one pushes it apart. They are FontLab's own numbers from the kerning table of the current master (master names it), class (group) kerning included. 'source' says which kind of entry gave the value: 'glyph : glyph' (a direct pair), 'glyph : class' or 'class : glyph' (an exception against a class, with right_class or left_class naming it), 'class : class' (both classes named), or 'none' (FontLab has no entry: value 0, kerned false). Report the source as given; never guess whether a value comes from a class. An entry can hold 0 on purpose (kerned false, but a source other than 'none'). A result with an 'error' was NOT read: never treat it as zero kerning. Other masters, feature-based (GPOS lookup) and contextual kerning are NOT covered by this tool. Only the pairs you asked for were read; do not request the whole table.

29d  agent_instruction         -> Tell the person that the glyph has OPEN contours: they have no fill, so they are strokes or construction lines, not filled shapes. Also tell them that in testing (FontLab 8.9.5, File > Export with the TrueType and the OpenType PS profiles, default settings) FontLab dropped open contours from the generated font without a warning: a glyph made only of open contours came out empty. To keep such a shape in a generated font, its contours must be closed or turned into outlines in FontLab first. Other export settings were not tested. The font was changed in FontLab's memory only: no Glyphbridge tool saves anything, saving is the person's job. Do NOT remind the person after every step. Tell the person these facts about kerning written this way: (1) FontLab applies the value in text at once, but its KERNING panel does not show it yet: a new pair is missing from the panel and a pair it already lists keeps its old value, until the person switches to another open font and back (with one font open: File > New Font, then back to this font's tab; the new font can be closed without saving) or saves and reopens the font. (3) Edit > Undo cannot take it back; to revert a glyph pair, if 'before' was a glyph : glyph entry call set_kerning again with that value; if it was 'none' or a class value, call remove_kerning to remove the new glyph : glyph entry and restore the prior state; a class pair: see below. Setting a pair to 0 leaves an explicit zero entry; remove_kerning removes the glyph : glyph entry. Other masters, other kerning entries and feature-based kerning were not changed. A pair with 'kind' has a kerning class side ('@name'): its entry is of that kind, and FontLab applies the value to every glyph pair of the classes' members that has no exception of its own ('classes' gives each class's side and member_count; those glyph pairs were not read). For such a pair 'before' and 'after' belong to ONE sample glyph pair ('sample'): sample_verdict 'applied' means the sample now takes the new value; 'covered' means the sample has an exception of its own, which still applies to it, and the class entry itself was confirmed by re-reading it; 'no sample' means no member of a class is a glyph of the font, so only the entry itself was re-read. Tell the person how far a class value reaches. To revert a class entry: if entry_before is a number, call set_kerning with that value for the same '@' pair; if it is null, call remove_kerning for the same '@' pair. The font was changed in FontLab's memory only: no Glyphbridge tool saves anything, saving is the person's job. Do NOT remind the person after every step.
